# RESEARCH_LOG — one row per pre-registered test, append-only

**Audit item M1.** Started 2026-08-03 while executing `VALQUO_EDGE_AUDIT.md`.

## Why this file exists

Every multiple-testing claim in this project is currently computed against a denominator of **8**
— the eight weight schemes `_deflated_sharpe` is handed. The external audit reconstructed roughly
**146 distinct pre-registered tests** across the handoff corpus. Those two facts cannot both
inform the same claim, and the smaller one is the one shipping in `BACKTEST_RESULTS.json`.

The purpose here is a single, honest trial counter that survives sessions, so that:

- `_deflated_sharpe` can be fed a real `N` instead of 8;
- Benjamini–Hochberg can be applied across the family of *equity* signal tests, the way
  `options_autopsy` already does across its 126 option features;
- the Harvey–Liu–Zhu adjusted hurdle can be quoted for the number of trials **actually run**.

At N ≈ 146, √(2·ln N) ≈ 3.16 — which is, near enough, the Harvey–Liu–Zhu hurdle of 3.0 that the
long-short *t* of 3.52 already clears. **That is a stronger claim than the current one precisely
because it is defensible**, which is the whole argument for maintaining this file.

> **CORRECTED 2026-08-15 (audit `MA5`). BOTH FIGURES IN THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE ARE NOW WRONG, AND
> THE CONCLUSION IS REVERSED.** (a) **There is no 3.0 hurdle.** The bar is `statistics.hlz_hurdle(N)`
> = √(2·ln N), and 3.0 is that expression frozen at N = 90 — a value this project passed on
> 2026-08-06. At today's equity **N = 224 the hurdle is 3.2899**, and it only ever rises.
> (b) **The 3.52 is the VOID pre-B6 panel.** The live long-short HAC *t* is **2.6199**, so it does
> **not** clear the hurdle — `R4` measured the shortfall at 0.66 and ships both sides of the
> argument in `BACKTEST_RESULTS.json.multiple_testing.hlz`. The paragraph is kept rather than
> deleted because it is the argument that built this file, and the argument was right: quoting the
> hurdle at the trials **actually run** is the defensible move. What it got wrong is that doing so
> makes the claim *harder*, not easier. **Derive the bar; never quote a constant for it.**

## Status — read this before using the count

**The retrospective population is NOT done, and `N` is NOT wired into `_deflated_sharpe`.**

The audit's own instruction was to populate this from "section A of the ledger", the reconstructed
list of ~146 prior tests. **That section is not in `VALQUO_EDGE_AUDIT.md` and no such document is
in the working folder** — only the count is quoted, in four places. So the retrospective rows have
to be re-extracted from the handoff corpus (`HANDOFF_*.md`, `OPTIONS_*.md`, the roadmap files),
which is real clerical work and was not attempted in a session already carrying thirteen
corrections.

**Wiring a partial count would be worse than the current state, not better.** A denominator of 30
carries the same false precision as a denominator of 8 while looking like it was measured. The
current statistic at least now *labels itself* as an undeflated PSR when `sr0` collapses (audit
B9), which is an honest signal. Wiring `N` happens when the count is complete, in one step.

## Schema

One row per test. A test earns a row when its threshold was committed **before** its run — that
is what makes it a trial rather than an observation. Exploratory looks, smoke tests and
diagnostics do **not** get rows; they get no claim either.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `id` | stable, never reused. `<domain><n>` or the audit's own item ID where one exists |
| `date` | ISO date the verdict was recorded |
| `domain` | `equity` / `options` / `unified` / `infra` — BH-FDR families are formed within a domain |
| `hypothesis` | what was predicted, in one line, in the direction predicted |
| `universe` | the exact set. "full 2,710-name panel", "187-name options book", "smoke test: 12 names" |
| `metric` | the statistic the verdict rests on |
| `threshold` | the bar, as committed **before** the run |
| `verdict` | `ADOPTED` / `REJECTED` / `NULL` / `INCONCLUSIVE` / `SUPERSEDED` / `FIXED` |
| `source` | the handoff or commit carrying the numbers |

`FIXED` marks a correctness repair rather than a hypothesis test. **`FIXED` rows do NOT count
toward `N`** — repairing a bug is not a search over the data, and inflating the denominator with
bug fixes would understate the evidence rather than overstate it.

**`SUPERSEDED` ROWS DO COUNT. Resolved 2026-08-06, session 7.** This paragraph used to end
"Only `ADOPTED` / `REJECTED` / `NULL` / `INCONCLUSIVE` rows are trials", which excluded
`SUPERSEDED` — and `research_log.py` has never implemented that, because `_parse` skips `FIXED`
and nothing else. The prose and the counter disagreed, and it was not academic: the void X3 run
carries `n=12`, so the reading decides whether equity `N` is 92 or 104. Session 6 hit the
discrepancy, took the harsher number, and referred the rule here rather than settling it while
looking at its own results.

**The counter is right and the prose was wrong.** `N` is a multiple-testing denominator, and what
inflates the best-looking result is how many times the data was searched — not how the search was
labelled afterwards. A superseded search still happened, and what it found still shaped what was
run next. `SUPERSEDED` judges the validity of a RESULT; it says nothing about whether the data was
interrogated. This is the same argument the module's own docstring already makes for counting
trials that were never pre-committed, applied consistently. It is also the conservative reading:
it makes `N` larger and every threshold harder to clear.

---

## How to add a row

1. Write the hypothesis and the threshold **before** the run, in the session handoff.
2. Run it.
3. Append one row here with the verdict — including, especially, the rejections and the nulls.
   A log that only records adoptions is a denominator of 8 with extra steps.
4. Never edit or delete a row. A superseded result gets a **new** row with verdict `SUPERSEDED`
   pointing at the one that replaced it.

---

## Rows — retrospective reconstruction (added 2026-08-05, audit M1)

**Counting rule (changed 2026-08-05, and the change matters):** a trial counts whether or not its
threshold was pre-committed. The original rule — "a row is earned when the threshold was committed
before the run" — is the right test for whether a RESULT is credible and the wrong one for a
multiple-testing DENOMINATOR. What inflates the best-looking result is how many times the data was
searched, not how well each search was documented. The old rule would have systematically
understated `N` and therefore OVERSTATED significance, which is the exact error M1 exists to fix.
`pre` records `yes` (threshold in writing beforehand) or `retro` (reconstructed from the record).
`FIXED` rows still do not count. `n=<k>` marks a pre-registered GRID of k cells counted as k.

This row is KEPT and STILL COUNTS: the arms were run, and the exploratory read they produced is exactly why the re-run was not blind and is charged again. A meter that has not crossed is therefore the EXPECTED outcome and is not evidence against the strategy. Sigma may never be revised downward - at 1.5x the assumed volatility the false-crossing rate is 20%. The limit is therefore set from a POST-CLOSE quote and the day order fills at the NEXT OPEN, so fill-vs-limit on this book is an OVERNIGHT GAP and not execution quality. Verdict on the live engine book pre-committed as NON-CONFORMING before measuring, in PREREG_session16_paper_track_repair.md section 4. Either the engine is aligned to the contract's construction, or the recorded days are registered as a separate experiment that may never be quoted as the Index. No third state | FIXED | n=1 | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md session 16; PAPER_TRACK_CONTRACT.md section 5b; tests/test_paper_track.py. THE ROUTED DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART. The split was reported as the engine violating the 8 pct cap by holding 10 names at 10 pct each. It is not a cap violation: valquo_index.build_index sets cap = max(MAX_WEIGHT, 1/len(picks)) with an explicit comment saying why - ten names at 8 pct sum to 80 pct - and the payload has always self-reported effective_max_weight. The weights were correct for the book they described. THE REAL DIVERGENCE IS BOOK SIZE, 10 names against the published Index's 86, and it is ONE construction fed TWO inputs: n = max(MIN_NAMES, round(len(large) * TOP_DECILE)) with MIN_NAMES = 10, so a 10-name book means the eligible large-cap tier held FEWER THAN 100 names while the published 86 implies a tier of roughly 860. /admin/run-paper-track reads data/valquo_index.json when it exists and SILENTLY REBUILDS FROM THE STORE'S LATEST SCAN when it does not, and that scan is a top-N hot list. The engine was never pointed at a different strategy; it was handed a truncated one with no label saying so. That is how PT-OUTBOUND happened - a Discord recap read the engine and published +0.18pp while the bound recorder read -2.85pp. ENFORCED: seed_book now REFUSES a non-conforming book and says why, loudly and non-destructively (the run continues and marks what is already held, because silently liquidating a live sandbox book on a conformance rule would be worse than the split). The only other way through is experiment=True, which STAMPS EVERY HOLDING ROW - on the row rather than in a return value, because PT-OUTBOUND's lesson is that the old code DID label its fallback honestly and no surface ever rendered the label. index_summary carries book_conformance measured FROM THE HOLDINGS rather than remembered from a seed run. The four recorded days (2026-08-03, 04, 05, 07; 10 names at 0.10) are REGISTERED AS A SEPARATE EXPERIMENT and KEPT, not deleted - deleting a record because it turned out to describe the wrong book is the flattering direction. Its FILLS remain real evidence about execution and are what V5 measures; its RETURN SERIES is evidence about nothing the contract binds. This does NOT open or close a vintage: the engine's series was never the bound series, so run #2's inception 2026-08-10, gate 2027-02-10 and verdict 2031-08-10 are untouched. STILL OPEN: the alignment is a GATE, not a repair - nothing here makes the engine start recording the real 86-name book, which needs a conforming data/valquo_index.json present on the Render disk when the cycle runs. Until then the gate's effect is that the engine stops ADDING to a non-conforming book. Equity N stays 131. |

Paired monthly difference d = live vintage excess minus shadow vintage excess, positive meaning the adoption helped. Robbins normal-mixture anytime-valid boundary, rho 3, alpha 0.05 two-sided, sigma = (annualised TE between the two books) / sqrt(12) * sqrt(1.466091), the AR(1) design effect, measured ONCE at pair open and never revised downward. NOT-COMPARABLE if weight overlap < 0.20 (checked first); INSUFFICIENT below 6 complete months; CONFIRMED-LIVE if cumulative difference exceeds the boundary; HARMED if it exceeds it downward; NULL otherwise, always quoted with the difference it could have detected | REGISTERED | n=1 | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md session 16; VALQUO_EXTENSIONS.md V1; valuation/edge/shadow_vintage.py; tests/test_shadow_vintage.py (26 tests, every frozen parameter pinned to a literal). NO MEASUREMENT AND NO VERDICT - this row is logged at REGISTRATION. GENUINELY BLIND, and more so than any previous register in this project: vintage 2 opened 2026-08-10 and has NO successor, so no vintage pair exists at all and not one parameter could have been chosen to suit a comparison, even in principle. Vintage 2's parameters are pinned NOW, in a tracked file, at params_id 0060c5ef3dda, so the shadow will run a pinned snapshot rather than a reconstruction. THE POWER IS STATED IN ADVANCE AND THE HONEST HALF IS UNFLATTERING: pairing is a large real gain - at an annualised between-book TE of 2.0 pp/yr the 60-month detectable difference is 3.34 pp/yr against the vs-SPY meter's 19.01 - but the tension is STRUCTURAL and no design escapes it. Sigma is small exactly when the two books overlap, i.e. when the adoption changed little; an adoption big enough to matter also raises sigma. So a shadow pair that has not crossed is the EXPECTED outcome and is NOT evidence that the adoption was worthless, and verdict() carries that sentence in its own output rather than leaving it in a markdown file. THE CONTROL IS EXACT: fed the vs-SPY meter's own 11.40 pp/yr tracking error this machinery reproduces the contract's published sigma 3.9847 and its published ~19 pp/yr at 60 months to the digit, because it IMPORTS track_meter.boundary rather than re-implementing it - one boundary function in the project, so the guarantee cannot change for one meter and not the other. Rho and alpha are imported, not re-declared, and a test fails if they are copied. The verdict has NO SIGN BRANCH - an AST test requires exactly one, the one that names the crossing direction - and a test flips the sign of an entire series and requires the verdict to flip with it and nothing else to move, so HARMED is exactly as reachable as CONFIRMED-LIVE. SCOPE, fenced before it has any numbers to leak: research instrumentation only, never any public surface, pinned by a walk over valuation/saas and valuation/web plus the templates - PT-OUTBOUND is why this fence exists in advance rather than afterwards. It does not touch the contract, cannot pass the operational gate, and does not weaken Amendment 1 rule 6: an adoption still resets the vintage clock. This measures the price; it does not refund it. A registered instrument that has produced no measurement searches nothing, so it is charged to infra at n=1 on the HACFLOOR / CHAINFREEZE precedent: equity N stays 131. The FIRST SHADOW PAIR is a trial and is charged when it opens, not now. |

Two pre-specified arms and NO selection step: A7 = deployed value quality momentum insider capital_discipline size institutional at 0.125 each; B4 = value quality momentum size at 0.125 each. Primary statistic is the PAIRED per-period difference of top-decile alpha, HAC lag 1, because both arms are scored on the same dates and differencing cancels the market move that dominates each level. MATERIAL if d_alpha <= -1.95pp (X7's calibrated top-decile alpha margin) AND paired HAC abs(t) >= 2.0; IMMATERIAL if d_alpha > -1.95pp AND paired HAC abs(t) < 2.0; anything else is a NULL. Reported separately: does B4 clear the calibrated absolute floors of 2.2837 (long-short HAC t) and 2.2913 (alpha HAC t) | IMMATERIAL ON COST, BUT THE LIVE BOOK FAILS THE CALIBRATED LONG-SHORT FLOOR | n=4 | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md session 17; data/free_analysis/LIVE_THEME_COST.json; scripts/live_theme_cost.py; tests/test_edge.py (4 new V2G tests). Corrected 2,531-name / 69-date panel, 113,945 rows, 2009-01-15 to 2026-01-28. THE INCUMBENT ARM REPRODUCES THE RECORD TO THE DIGIT, which is the harness control: A7 gives top_decile_alpha 0.071741423321 (+7.17%), long-short naive t 2.8361, HAC t 2.6199, alpha HAC t 4.3762, monotonicity -0.891 - every one matching the figures CLAUDE.md already carries. RESULT: B4 alpha +5.86% against A7's +7.17%, so d_alpha = -1.3133pp against the -1.95pp bar, and the paired HAC t is -1.4040 over 69 paired dates. Both conditions of the IMMATERIAL branch are met, so the pre-registered verdict is IMMATERIAL and the cost of the three dead themes is NOT separable from zero. THE POWER CAVEAT MUST TRAVEL WITH THAT VERDICT: the HAC standard error of the annualised paired difference is 0.9354pp, so the smallest gap this design can detect at abs(t)=2 is 1.8708pp - well matched to the 1.95pp bar it committed to, but the power to detect a TRUE 1.95pp gap is only 55.0%. IMMATERIAL here means the cost could not be separated from zero at a coin-flip's power against its own bar, NOT that the cost was shown to be small. THE SECOND FINDING IS THE MORE SERIOUS ONE AND IT IS WHY THE REGISTER ASKED FOR IT SEPARATELY: the live four-theme book does NOT clear the calibrated long-short floor. B4's long-short HAC t is 1.8811 against the 2.2837 floor and its naive t is 2.0044 against 2.1437 - it fails both - where the deployed seven-theme book clears at 2.6199. B4 DOES clear the top-decile alpha HAC floor, 3.2087 against 2.2913, and since the shipped product is a LONG-ONLY hot list that is the product-relevant statistic. So the long-only book users receive remains demonstrable against a calibrated bar; the long-short research statistic it is quoted alongside is not. The paired long-short difference is -3.00pp/yr at HAC t -1.8792, closer to significance than the alpha difference. BOTH SPLIT DIRECTIONS AGREE IN SIGN AND NEITHER IS SIGNIFICANT: early half d_alpha -1.14pp (paired HAC t -1.3519, 34 dates), late half -1.48pp (t -0.8984, 35 dates). Unlike session 7's LOO arms, four of seven of which changed sign between halves, this delta is stable in direction. CONTROLS. C1 and C2 are EXACT at 0.000e+00 over all 113,945 rows: scoring the SEVEN-theme weight vector on a panel where the three dead themes are NaN (C1), and on one where insider is CONSTANT and the other two absent (C2, the live condition exactly), both reproduce the four-theme arm name for name. That is what makes B4 the live book rather than a model of it, and it holds because composite renormalises by the present-weight mass and zscore returns all-NaN on a zero-variance column. C3: the calibrated floors were measured at N=121 and re-verified at N=129, and adoption is monotone decreasing in N, so an identical adopter set means identical scores and identical percentiles - recomputed from the banked X7_RECONCILE margins, 20 adopters at N=129 and 20 at N=135, set identical, so the floors still stand at this N. The two arms scored the SAME 69 dates and the same median 1,557 names, so the register's stated risk that B4 could rank fewer names did not materialise. EXPLORATORY DECOMPOSITION, NO VERDICT, and pre-registered as carrying none because session 7 established on this exact panel that a full-sample ablation arm is not a finding: dropping insider alone gives +0.30% on the full sample but FLIPS SIGN across halves (-1.39% early, +1.94% late), consistent with the record's own note that insider's t is not a measurable quantity; dropping capital_discipline gives +1.37% full and is POSITIVE in both halves (+0.20% early, +2.51% late), i.e. the theme with the second-strongest panel IC (+2.76) and one of only two clearing X7's 2.71 bar appears to cost nothing to lose - a direct restatement of X3's finding that theme IC does not predict marginal contribution, and its early-half +0.20% coincides with the value session 7's held-out LOO measured for the same arm; dropping institutional is the ONLY one negative in both halves (-1.41% full, -0.89% early, -1.91% late). If one live source is built, 13F is the one the evidence points at, and that sentence is a build-priority hint, not a result. WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY, and it is the part most likely to be misquoted: an immaterial ALPHA cost is not a finding that the live absence is acceptable. The live product computes a different composite from the one every published figure is measured on, which is a claims-integrity issue independent of return - audit B7 exists because live and backtest scoring diverged before. Equity N 131 -> 135, charged as four arms (B4 plus the three decomposition arms); the halves are the same arms on subsets rather than new hypotheses and are not charged, on session 7's precedent of charging 7 for 7 LOO arms measured in both directions. WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY, and it is the most likely misuse: it is NOT a finding that the book should rebalance less often. cum_alpha(H) is the buy-and-hold return of the cohort selected on ONE date, whereas a quarterly-rebalanced book re-selects every quarter and compounds fresh selections, so those are different claims and only the first is measured. THREE OF THE FOUR FLIP SIGN BETWEEN HALVES: A1_FV_POINT early -0.22pp late -0.02pp; A2_FV_LENSBAND early -0.31pp late +1.04pp; A3_TPSL_ONEIL early -0.72pp late +0.21pp; A4_TPSL_2TO1 early -0.65pp late +0.30pp; C_NEVER early -2.09pp late -18.07pp - and the ONLY arm with a positive full-sample difference, A2 at +0.37pp/yr, is exactly the one that fails the both-halves requirement (negative early, positive late), which is session 7's LOO pattern again and precisely what that requirement exists to catch. THE CONTROL IS THE ONE LARGE AND WELL-MEASURED RESULT AND IT CONFIRMS S22 RATHER THAN CONTRADICTING IT: never selling costs -10.89pp/yr at HAC t -3.8013, the book grows to 417 names and alpha versus the equal-weighted universe collapses from 15.48% to 3.37%. That is NOT evidence against S22's persistence finding: S22 measured the forward return of a cohort selected on ONE date and found it beats the universe for about eight quarters, whereas a never-sell book keeps BUYING and so accumulates cohorts of every age - over 69 rebalances the average holding is far older than eight quarters and the book converges toward a slice of the universe whose alpha must approach the universe's own. Costs are not the story either, since C-NEVER's gross 20.61% barely differs from its net because it never sells; the mechanism is DILUTION, not friction, which is why the register labelled it a control and not a candidate in advance. CALIBRATED FLOOR BUILT RATHER THAN BORROWED, because X7 and session 10 calibrate quantile_backtest on the full-universe DECILE book and _backtest_hold is a different object: 200 placebo draws, seeds 3000-3199, through the identical arms, giving per-arm p95 floors of A1_FV_POINT 1.9181, A2_FV_LENSBAND 1.8518, A3_TPSL_ONEIL 2.0260, A4_TPSL_2TO1 2.0489, C_NEVER 2.5543; labelled fixed_weights_null and NOT comparable with 2.2837 or 2.2913. CONTROLS ALL PASS. C1, the valuation panel lands on the factor panel's dates EXACTLY (69 dates, 2009-01-15 to 2026-01-28) and covers 93.9% of its rows. C2, ZERO network calls during the valuation build, enforced by a tripwire that raises. C3, the incumbent reproduces the shipped _backtest_hold to sixteen digits, total_return 158.09816857704106. C4, every arm scores identical dates and target_n. C6, net is at most gross for every arm and the turnover ranking and the drag ranking are IDENTICAL. Every exit rule genuinely fires, so no arm is a silent copy of the incumbent. Fair-value coverage reported before any verdict per the COVERAGE RULE: 108,100 of 108,241 rows valuable, the point gate firing on 60,526 and the lens gate on 76,320. THE LEVELS ARE NOT A NEW HEADLINE: _backtest_hold is the concentrated top-25-to-50 book audit B17 already labels the noisiest number in the results file, whose realised size is about exit_rank rather than top_n. This register measures DIFFERENCES between exit rules on that object; the incumbent's own level is not a claim it makes. THE TP/SL LIMITATION TRAVELS WITH THE ARMS: the panel carries no intra-quarter path, so a name that touches +40% mid-quarter and gives it back is never seen to hit a +25% take-profit, and those rules therefore trigger LESS often than real ones - which biases the measurement IN FAVOUR of the TP/SL arms and makes a negative result on them the trustworthy direction. EXPECTATIONS: no challenger beating the incumbent was predicted at 75/25 and is RIGHT; TP/SL being worse than the fair-value arms was predicted at 60/40 and is RIGHT directionally though every margin is inside noise; all four price exits being negative was predicted at 70/30 and is WRONG since A2 is positive; C-NEVER beating the incumbent gross was predicted at 55/45 and is WRONG by 13.5pp; fair-value coverage below 90% was predicted at 60/40 and is WRONG at 93.9%; and whether the stop-loss or the take-profit does the damage is recorded UNRESOLVED rather than scored, because A3 and A4 differ in BOTH legs at once and adding a TP-only or SL-only arm after seeing this result is exactly the grid search the register forbids. Equity N 143 -> 149, charged as five arms plus the control; the placebo calibration, the half-splits and the coverage diagnostics are charged at zero. ADOPTS NOTHING - adoption is a VINTAGE EVENT that resets the five-year clock for zero statistical gain and is Don's call on this evidence, not a consequence of a number clearing a bar. |

NOT REPAIRED because zscore is on the live scoring path and changing it is a scoring change and therefore a vintage event, which is not this register's to make; it is pinned by a test that fails if it is ever silently corrected. Scoped by a fact measured and disclosed BEFORE the register was written: bs_price, implied_vol and greeks ALREADY take a continuous dividend yield q and handle it correctly, so the defect is not a missing model but that every caller uses the default q of 0.0. The banked P&L comes from QUOTED bid and ask in the frozen chains and never from a model price, so the pricer cannot move it directly; it reaches the book through exactly three doors, measured separately. D1 early exercise, the sim always SELLS at the bid while a deep-ITM American call near ex-div can be worth more exercised. D2 contract selection, pick_contract takes the contract whose absolute delta is nearest 0.35 and delta is computed at q of zero. D3 stored derived fields. EARLY EXERCISE IS MEASURED MODEL-FREE, deliberately: the textbook condition compares the dividend to remaining time value, which would make the answer a function of the very pricer under test, so the test is simply whether the booked exit price is below intrinsic. PRIMARY yield is trailing-twelve-month dividends over spot, strictly point-in-time; a scheduled-dividends variant is defined but forbidden from carrying a verdict because it reads the contract's future. MATERIALITY BAR fixed in advance: FIX the pricer if the combined effect on per-trade expectancy is at least 1.00pp in absolute value, OR if any published verdict changes its relationship to its bar, with the second clause governing even if the first fails | IMMATERIAL ON THE MEASURABLE PART, and the D2 contribution is UNRESOLVED rather than zero - the pricer is deliberately NOT changed | n=1 | HANDOFF_optionsbot.md section 29; data/free_analysis/O21_DIVIDENDS.json; scripts/o21_dividends.py; valuation/edge/dividends.py; tests/test_dividends.py 27 tests. EXPOSURE IS REAL AND WIDESPREAD: 81.4 pct of the 3,870 banked trades sit on dividend payers at a median trailing yield of 2.02 pct and a maximum of 24.8 pct, and 2,107 of 3,870 calls, 54.4 pct, span an ex-dividend date. THE COST IS NOT. D1: only 34 of 3,870 exits, 0.879 pct, were booked below intrinsic, worth +0.2002pp of per-trade expectancy against a 1.00pp bar. TWO CONTROLS, BOTH RUN BEFORE ANY FIGURE WAS QUOTED: put-call parity recovers the stored underlying_entry with a median relative error of 0.00232 and 93.2 pct within one percent, and the bars raw_close the simulation itself uses reproduces it with a median relative error of 0.00000 and 100.0 pct within one percent. D2: the q equals zero arm reproduces the banked contract on 3,870 of 3,870 entries, exactly 100 pct, which is what makes the challenger quotable, and the corrected pricer selects a DIFFERENT contract on 179 entries, 4.63 pct. The substitution is NOT a near-substitute, which makes the unresolved part more serious rather than less: median absolute delta gap 0.129 against a 0.35 target, p90 0.225, 87.7 pct keep the same expiry and 93.9 pct move to a LOWER strike, which is the predicted direction since a positive q lowers call delta and the selector must go further in the money to reach 0.35. D2'S P&L IS NOT COMPUTABLE ON THE FROZEN BOOK AND THAT IS REPORTED RATHER THAN ASSUMED TO BE ZERO: the freeze stores the FULL chain only on ENTRY dates and just the traded contract thereafter, so a contract the book never held has no forward price path - the median alternative has 2 chain dates and only 10.1 pct have more than three. Resolving it needs a re-mine, which this session was scoped out of. D3: ignoring dividends UNDERSTATES the solved implied vol by a median 0.00617, about 0.62 vol points, and OVERSTATES absolute delta by a median 0.00668, with maxima of 0.0998 and 0.146; the path study's delta85 arm moves from firing on 6 trades to firing on 2 of 3,870, which cannot disturb its recorded -0.02pp rejection. THE PRICER IS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED, because neither clause of the pre-registered bar is met and shipping a selection change on an unmet bar is exactly what the register exists to prevent - passing q into pick_contract would alter WHICH CONTRACT the live engine buys on 4.63 pct of entries, which is a construction change and not a bug fix. That non-change is PINNED by two tests that fail if the live selection path ever silently starts passing a yield, the same discipline session 16 used for the sizing quantity and session 20 for zscore. TWO DEFECTS IN MY OWN INSTRUMENT, both caught before any verdict was read and both pinned by tests. First, spot at exit was initially estimated as the maximum of call bid plus strike over the chain; parity gives call at least spot minus strike, so every bid plus strike is an UPPER bound on spot and the maximum is the loosest one available, which inflated intrinsic and reported an early-exercise gain of +5.62pp on a smoke subset - eight times the true figure and in the direction that would have manufactured a material finding. Replaced by put-call parity, an equality rather than a bound. Second, the parity helper then rejected a put mid at or below zero, which discards exactly the deep-ITM-call cases early exercise lives in and scored ZERO rows. Also found: options_backtest.BARS_CACHE is a RELATIVE path, so it silently resolves to nothing from a git worktree and returned an empty bar set rather than an error. EXPECTATIONS scored 4 right and 1 wrong of 5 scored: IMMATERIAL predicted at 70/30 is RIGHT on the measurable part; fewer than 2 pct of exits below intrinsic predicted at 65/35 is RIGHT at 0.879 pct; selection changing on fewer than 10 pct of entries predicted at 55/45 is RIGHT at 4.63 pct; the delta85 firing rate moving by less than 1pp predicted at 75/25 is RIGHT at 0.10pp; and the implied-vol shift being NEGATIVE is WRONG AS STATED - the register did not state the direction of subtraction, and the underlying arithmetic it gave is confirmed, namely that ignoring dividends understates the solved vol, so the shift measured as corrected minus published is POSITIVE. | O26 | 2026-08-11 | options | yes | options_tracker.MIN_CLOSED_PER_BUCKET is 30 and gates whether a subgroup may be tuned on or reported as judgeable. Its own comment states the purpose - enough that one lucky contract cannot flip the verdict - and that property has never been measured. At what bucket size does it actually hold | PREREG_o26_bucket_floor.md, committed ALONE with PREREG_o21_dividends.md at bf5324c before any measurement code existed. PRIMARY STATISTIC is the literal operationalisation of the constant's own comment: P_flip of n, the probability that removing the SINGLE most extreme trade flips the sign of a size-n bucket's mean. The removed trade is argmax of the absolute deviation from the mean, chosen WITHOUT reference to which way it moves the mean - removing the best trade instead would build the answer's direction into its own definition. Estimated by drawing size-n buckets with replacement from the split-clean alert book, 5000 draws per n, over a grid of 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 75, 100, 150, 200 and 300 fixed in advance and not extendable after seeing the curve. FLOOR is the smallest n on the grid with P_flip at most 0.05. RAISE iff the floor computed independently on the early and late calendar halves agrees within one grid step and exceeds 30; KEEP 30 iff the floor is at most 30 on both halves; NULL otherwise, and a NULL keeps the shipped value so the failure direction is always keep 30 rather than adopt an unvalidated number. SECONDARY and independent: half-to-half sign agreement of bucket expectancy, reported separately rather than folded into the primary | NULL, so the floor STAYS 30 - but not because 30 is adequate; because NO floor available to this book delivers the property the constant claims | n=1 | HANDOFF_optionsbot.md section 30; data/free_analysis/O26_BUCKET_FLOOR.json; scripts/o26_floor.py; valuation/edge/bucket_floor.py; tests/test_bucket_floor.py 19 tests. P_flip NEVER REACHES THE 0.05 BAR ANYWHERE ON THE GRID, on the full sample or on either half: 0.2226 at n=10, 0.1848 at the shipped n=30, 0.1672 at 50, 0.1440 at 100, 0.1318 at 150 and still 0.1084 at n=300. So the verdict is NULL by the pre-committed rule and the constant is left at 30, but the honest reading is that raising it would buy very little - going from 30 to 300, a tenfold increase no live bucket could supply, moves the flip probability only from 0.185 to 0.108. THE SECONDARY IS THE STRONGER RESULT AND IT IS DEVASTATING: half-to-half sign agreement is a COIN FLIP at every bucket size tested, 0.4942 at n=30, 0.5098 at 100, 0.5212 at 150 and 0.5482 at 300, against a 0.95 target it never approaches. THAT IS NOT A SIMULATION ARTEFACT AND IT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: the book's own moments, a mean of 0.0327 and a per-trade standard deviation of 0.9251, predict agreement of 0.5059 at n=30, 0.5289 at 150 and 0.5561 at 300 in closed form, against measured values of 0.4942, 0.5212 and 0.5482. The simulation reproduces first-principles theory across the whole grid. THE NUMBER THAT MATTERS FOR EVERY FUTURE SUBGROUP CLAIM: with that mean and that dispersion a bucket needs roughly 6,148 trades before its two halves would agree on the SIGN of expectancy 95 pct of the time. The entire banked book is 3,870 and the largest live bucket, large-cap, is 2,058, with mega at 994, mid at 774 and small at 44. So per-bucket expectancy on this book is essentially unmeasurable at any floor, and the floor is doing far less work than its comment implies. This corroborates R2 and O13 from a third direction rather than adding a new claim. ZERO live buckets change status, because the verdict is NULL and the floor is unchanged. NOTHING SHIPS: MIN_CLOSED_PER_BUCKET is untouched at 30, and the item may not change live trading behaviour in any case since raising it only makes the project MORE conservative about which subgroups it will read. EXPECTATIONS scored 2 right and 3 wrong: a floor in the 75 to 150 range predicted at 70/30 is WRONG since no floor exists on the grid at all; a RAISE verdict predicted at 60/40 is WRONG since it is NULL; at least one currently-reported bucket losing enough_to_judge predicted at 65/35 is WRONG at zero; P_flip at the shipped 30 exceeding 0.15 predicted at 70/30 is RIGHT at 0.1848; and the secondary criterion being weaker than the primary predicted at 55/45 is RIGHT and by a wide margin, since the primary at least declines with n while the secondary never leaves the neighbourhood of a coin flip. THE QUANTITY is rho, the effective half-spread divided by the quoted half-spread, so rho of 1 is the engine's assumption and rho below 1 means it overcharges; aggregated size-weighted to the contract-day then averaged equally across contract-days, with the unweighted version reported alongside. TWO SPREAD LEVELS ARE KEPT DELIBERATELY APART because conflating them is the obvious error: the EOD snapshot the book charges and the quote prevailing when trades actually happen. The gap between them is an AVAILABILITY term that is SELECTED, since you only avoid it if you trade when the market is there, and the register forbids quoting it as a saving; only the price-improvement term is a property of execution. SIX CONDITIONING FAMILIES fixed in advance and no others, each cut into quintiles: quoted spread-pct, entry premium, dte, market cap, minutes from the open and print size. opt_right and horizon are excluded as degenerate, the banked book being 100 pct calls and 100 pct swing. WARRANTED iff some family's range clears its own half's p95 in BOTH halves AND the Spearman ordering agrees in sign; a family clearing full-sample but not both halves is reported and not acted on. So A3's -11.1pp is an EXPOSURE effect wearing a cheapness rule's name, and the audit's claim that this cleanly separates which NAME from which CONTRACT is wrong: on this candidate set you cannot change the contract on a cheapness criterion without changing the exposure. A COROLLARY THAT LIMITS A1: its delta gap of 0.004 means the plus-or-minus-0.05 band pins it to essentially the incumbent contract, so A1's near-zero gain is close to tautological and is NOT evidence that cheapness is uninformative. THE PRE-REGISTERED RANDOM-ENTRY CONTROL ARM, which carries no verdict and cost zero trials, ANSWERS THE PORTABILITY QUESTION AND IS THE MOST USEFUL RESULT HERE: on the five-seed random-entry control book the identical four rules reproduce the same ordering and nearly the same magnitudes - A1 +0.057pp, A2 -4.140pp, A3 -9.382pp, A4 +0.310pp over 5,984 events - so this is a property of CONTRACT SELECTION generally and not of the dead alert days, and it is portable to any future book. EXPECTATIONS E1 no arm reaches CANDIDATE at 65/35 is RIGHT; E2 that a raw improvement would appear but be killed by the null at 70/30 is WRONG in an instructive way, because the null is far BELOW zero and the two positive arms clear it easily - what kills A4 is the tail clause, not the null. Measured against the book's own underlying_entry over 1,173 banked entries, raw_close agrees EXACTLY at median relative error 0.00000 with nothing over 5 pct off, while the adjusted close is off by a median 10.3 pct and by more than 5 pct on 67 pct of entries. The first cut reported a mean implied move of 19.57 pct against a realised 5.26 pct and a confident RICH verdict; that was an ARTEFACT, since 2.82 pct of events sat more than 20 pct from the money and up to 30x off, making those straddles almost pure intrinsic. Repaired to raw_close for anything touching a STRIKE and adjusted close only for a RETURN, which cannot fake a move when a split lands inside the window; coverage rose 0.2738 to 0.4459 and the implied move fell to a credible 5.45 pct. A control that must stay empty now records any raw close disagreeing with the book's own spot and reads ZERO, and three tests pin the distinction. SECOND: the first expiry rule took the nearest expiry with DTE at least 7, which prices the move TO EXPIRY over 30-plus days against a realised move measured over about four, a tenor mismatch that also inflates implied move; repaired to the first expiry strictly AFTER the announcement and no more than 45 days out. THIRD, a copy-paste: B2's confidence interval was the diagnostic's bootstrap, so two different quantities reported bit-identical intervals. A concurrency cap therefore refuses trades exactly when the opportunity is richest - 1,677 of 3,870 skipped on concurrency at cap 10 - which is why the constrained small-capital book is not merely more volatile but outright loss-making. SWEEP SHARE IS THE CLOSEST THING THE OPTIONS PROGRAMME HAS PRODUCED TO A DISCOVERY, AND IT IS STILL A NULL. Its sign is STABLE across halves at -0.1689 early and -0.1086 late, and the negative sign means HIGH sweep share earns MORE - the institutional-FOLLOW direction rather than the retail-FADE one, which is informative precisely because the audit said the sign could not be predicted. | X1 | 2026-08-13 | equity | yes | Audit row X1, split on the UNIVERSE rather than on TIME. Every held-out gate this project has ever run splits by DATES, so the headline has never been tested on an independence axis with no regime confound - which is the audit's own argument for calling X1 possibly the highest-value methodological change in the document, since a time split conflates whether the SIGNAL generalises with whether the PERIOD does. THE EFFECT IS REAL AND SURVIVES THE ALERT'S DEATH. On 27,350 random-entry trades a call spanning the next announcement earns +10.30% against +5.50%, a +4.79pp gain, positive in BOTH halves (+5.03 / +4.70), paired name-year sign test z +2.054 p 0.040. So owning an earnings event is the property, not the alert. AND THE ALERT STILL SUBTRACTS VALUE INSIDE THE SUBSET THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO RESCUE IT: alert-spanning +8.42% LOSES to random-spanning +10.30% at paired sign z -4.4726, p 7.7e-06 over 952 cells - R2 reproduced inside the earnings subset - so the strategy is 'buy calls spanning earnings' with no alert in it at all. IT IS A MEAN EFFECT AND NOT A MEDIAN EFFECT: DTE-matched at +/-5 days on the same name the mean gain clears a shuffled-label null on both books (+9.10pp vs p95 +4.00pp alert, +2.51pp vs +1.44pp random) while median-vs-median is +0.40pp, spanning -51.41% against -51.81%. The typical trade is a near-total loss either way, which is U1's finding restated and the reason a positive expectancy here is not tradeability. THREE EXACT CONTROLS: A1's spans_mean 0.084199 reproduces O17's kept_mean to the digit, the implied all-book mean 0.03734 reproduces its all_mean exactly, and the pooled random book lands at +8.47% against R2's banked +8.3342%. A DEFECT IN MY OWN BARS, FOUND BY RUNNING THEM, AND IT DECIDES THE REGISTERED VERDICT: two of the three do not measure what they were written to. B1's trades-per-year axis is UNPASSABLE BY CONSTRUCTION for a subset of the book it is drawn from, and B1's and B2's NAME axes re-measure the earnings coverage hole - 29 of 186 names are foreign private issuers with zero earnings dates, so the spanning set is capped at 157 names before any concentration exists and it touches ALL 157, i.e. 100% of eligible names and 118 of 118 months. Only B3 measured its property and it PASSES, refuting the prediction that it would bind: the strategy is LESS concurrent than the alert book, peak 37 vs 48 and refused at O11's cap of 10 on 34.1% vs 48.7%. The register is left UNEDITED and the diagnosis ships beside it as a post-hoc field feeding no pass/fail flag, pinned by a test asserting all_bars_pass is assigned BEFORE the diagnostic exists. So a result that was NULL only because of a bar set for a product reason is now REJECTED only because of a bar that was broken, which is worth more than either verdict. A SECOND DEFECT, and it had already been reported once as a finding before it was caught: the DTE-matched MEDIAN was first read as -35.8pp and described as the typical spanning trade doing worse than its matched counterpart. It is an ARTIFACT - comparing one draw against the MEAN of a right-skewed set is biased low by construction, and the shuffled null median is -41.9pp against the real -35.8pp, so the real figure runs slightly IN THE ARM'S FAVOUR and the reading was exactly backwards. It is kept in the payload beside its own null with a note never to quote it alone. GOVERNING CAVEAT: O11 - a book with +3.27%/trade positive expectancy still ended at $37,059 from $50,000 at a concurrency cap of 10 - so nothing here licenses trading it, and MA54-2 is answered on its own terms and is Don's to close. | MB1 | 2026-08-19 | options | PREREG_mb1_alternatives_menu.md (committed ALONE at 33ad7ee, markdown only, a strict ancestor of every measurement commit) | Every options study this project has run scored the ONE contract the book held, so R2's -5.0640pp gap is a compound: the alert chose a DAY and a rule chose a CONTRACT, and the loss cannot be attributed to either. Score the WHOLE in-band menu on alert days against the same menu construction on R2's own five split-clean random-entry days. If the two menu distributions coincide the loss is 100% TIMING and contract selection is irrelevant; if the alert-day menu is systematically worse the alert is picking bad CHAINS rather than bad DAYS. Menu definition taken VERBATIM from the shipped pick_contract prefilter including its moneyness fallback - calls, DTE in 45 to 75, moneyness 0.90 to 1.20, solvable delta, and quote_reject_reason None - so it is the engine's own menu and the shipped pick is by construction a member of it. Both arms simulated through the SHIPPED simulate_trade on the PINNED harvest freeze. | PRIMARY the POOLED menu MEDIAN holding-period return, with the pooled menu p75 as the DECLARED SECONDARY carrying no independent verdict; the entry-weighted variant is reported with NO verdict and may never be quoted as the primary. KILL CONDITION AS WRITTEN: if the alert-day and random-day pooled menu medians differ by LESS THAN 1.0pp in EITHER half, contract selection is declared IRRELEVANT and no further contract-selection register may be opened on this book, permanently. The condition fires on the WEAKER of the two halves, so a 3pp gap in one half and a 0.4pp gap in the other FIRES it; softening either half to both is a void condition. A pass does not license a trade because O11 binds. Delta buckets 0.00 to 0.15, 0.15 to 0.35, 0.35 to 0.60 and 0.60 up, chosen from the measured delta distribution and carrying NO verdict. TWO GATING CONTROLS IN THEIR OWN PASS with arms refusing without a passing artifact: C1 the menu must contain the shipped pick and its own argmin must BE that pick on at least 99% of covered alert entries; C2 the two arms' coverage shares must lie within 2.0pp. | KILL FIRES as registered - and the closure is NOT SOUND, on three measurements of the same data | n=2 | PREREG_mb1_alternatives_menu.md; HANDOFF_optionsbot.md 66 (pending). PREMISE CORRECTION FIXED BEFORE ANY OUTCOME: the audit's 636 alternatives per entry date is the WHOLE CHAIN, and the engine's own in-band fillable menu has a median of FIVE. Measured stage by stage on 80 sampled covered entries - 864 raw, 432 calls, 31 in the DTE band, 10 in the moneyness band, 9 with a solvable delta, 5 fillable - so the DTE band does most of the cutting and the fillability filter removes nearly half of what survives, refusing on low_volume 53.5%, wide_spread 44.1% and thin_premium 2.4%. That is MA31's pair-availability warning and it is far more severe than the item anticipated, so any reading leaning on a distribution over 636 is void; the arms still pool about 12,000 and 93,000 legs, which is ample for a 1.0pp bar. COVERAGE PARITY IS THE POINT AND IT HOLDS: alert 2,446 of 3,870 = 63.2% against control 18,531 of 29,654 = 62.5%, a 0.7pp gap, so the comparison is not confounded by differential coverage. The menu's median delta is 0.470 against the 0.35 the engine targets, so the menu is NOT centred on the pick and a delta axis is needed rather than decorative. AND THE PINNED ARTIFACT MOVED: O21-D2 recorded the harvest freeze at manifest sha db7f0f84 with 1,865 units generated 2026-08-18T10:28:29Z, and the SAME root now reads sha ee6d38e5 with 2,850 units generated 2026-08-19T03:39:52Z - a dated freeze rewritten a day later, which is the O16 failure the pin exists to prevent, caught only because the fingerprint is recorded rather than the label. RESULT. The registered primary is the pooled menu MEDIAN and its gaps are -0.7310pp early and -0.5366pp late, both inside the 1.0pp bar, so the kill FIRES and the arms pass records CLOSED - contract selection is IRRELEVANT. THAT CLOSURE IS NOT SOUND AND THE EVIDENCE IS ON THE SAME DATA. First, THE REGISTERED STATISTIC IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND: both arms' menu medians sit at about -0.50, so the typical menu leg is a near-total loss on BOTH books and the median is pinned in that dense loss mass where it cannot see the right tail an option book's expectancy is built from - and O17C4 had ALREADY measured exactly this on this book, recording that the effect is a MEAN effect and not a MEDIAN one, so the register chose the one statistic the project had already shown cannot detect it. Second, A CLUSTERED PAIRED BOOTSTRAP ON R3's OWN NAME-YEAR CELL DOES NOT RESOLVE THE RULE IN ANY WINDOW: CI95 is [-13.8011, +15.7701] early, [-1.2154, +0.0922] late and [-7.0143, +0.1521] full, so the permanent closure rests on point estimates indistinguishable from gaps an order of magnitude larger, and the early interval spans 29.6pp around a -0.73pp estimate. Third, THE DECOMPOSITION THE ITEM EXISTS TO PRODUCE CONTRADICTS THE VERDICT: re-derived on exactly the covered entries, the picked-contract gap of -6.0326pp splits into a DAY effect of -4.7564pp (78.8%) and a SELECTION residual of -1.2762pp (21.2%), same sign in both halves at -1.7223pp early and -1.1450pp late - so contract selection carries about a fifth of the loss and its residual is LARGER than the 1.0pp bar the register itself set for materiality. AND THE KILL CONDITION'S INFERENCE IS INVERTED RELATIVE TO THE ITEM'S OWN DECOMPOSITION LOGIC: under the identity pick_gap = menu_gap + selection_residual, menus that COINCIDE mean the day effect is nil and the ENTIRE loss is selection, so a small menu gap implies selection carries everything rather than nothing. The rule was registered verbatim from the audit as the brief required, and running it verbatim is what surfaced that it does not follow. THE BEST AVAILABLE ANSWER to the item's actual question is therefore TIMING about 79 percent and SELECTION about 21 percent on the covered subset, not a closure. Controls: C1 the menu contains the shipped pick on 2,446 of 2,446 covered entries and the menu's own argmin IS that pick at 1.0000, so it is the engine's menu and not a reconstruction; C2 coverage parity 0.7134pp against a 2.0pp tolerance; C7, a control the register did not anticipate because the control books store no underlying_entry, reproduces the alert book's stored value EXACTLY on 2,446 of 2,446 at median relative error 0.000e+00. Menu depth is near-identical across the arms at 6.816 against 6.771 legs, so no gap here is a composition artefact. 16,672 alert legs over 2,446 entries against 123,415 control legs over 18,227. The 80-percent figure is (crit + 0.84) times se, 1.42x larger at crit 2.0 - so S19's +0.020549 becomes +0.029180 and V2G's 1.8708pp becomes 2.6565pp. Neither number is wrong as stated and quoting one as the other is. A design whose detection threshold sits just below the effect it is testing has about a coin flip of seeing it, which is exactly what a 50-percent-power threshold means. WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT SHIPPED, and the refusal is MB30's with MA21's precedent binding: no sweep over the register corpus, no warning, no build failure. About 68 historical registers state no MDE in this form, so such a check would fire on essentially all of them on its first run and be switched off inside a week - MA21 declined the identical shape once already, a blank-verdict warning that would have fired on 41 legitimate ledger rows. A rule that flags a known-good case is broken, so the rejection rate ships as the decision rule and the misspecified quantile is carried beside it, marked. ARM: multiply a flagged top-decile holding's weight by 0.5, the haircut FIXED before the run and NOT swept, weights renormalised so the book stays fully invested. GUARD RAIL, GATING AND ABLE TO REJECT ALONE: top-decile alpha NON-INFERIORITY against X7's calibrated 1.8629pp margin - if the arm's alpha is worse by more than that the arm is REJECTED regardless of the crash result, because a risk control that costs alpha is a trade and this register may not make it. THE GUARD RAIL WAS CHECKED FIRST AND PASSES - the arm gives up 0.1499pp annualised against the calibrated 1.8629pp margin - so the arm is not rejected on alpha; it is killed on the crash bar. THE FINDING: MA28's FLAG IS REAL ON THE PANEL AND NEARLY ABSENT FROM THE BOOK. The register's arithmetic said a 0.5x haircut removes at most half the crash exposure it touches, so clearing 20 percent needs the flagged names to carry at least 40 percent of the book's crash exposure; MA28's panel-wide figure is 19.14 percent, and IN THE TOP DECILE IT IS 1.19 PERCENT - one crash of eighty-four, with the early half at exactly ZERO flagged crashes in 34 dates, so the arithmetic ceiling on the reduction is 0.595 percent full sample and exactly zero early. The C5 census on 11,426 top-decile holdings, the same count S10 and V6-B's C7 report independently: flagged 407 rows 3.56 percent with 1 crash at rate 0.246 percent; flaggable-and-kept 8,081 rows 70.72 percent with 52 crashes at 0.643 percent; UNFLAGGABLE 2,938 rows 25.71 percent with 31 crashes at 1.055 percent. So the flag fires on 3.56 percent of the book against 5.74 percent of the panel, and what it flags accounts for 1.19 percent of the book's crashes against 19.14 percent panel-wide - the composite's top decile is quality and megacap tilted while MA28's flags fire on distressed aggressively-accounted names the composite has ALREADY declined to hold. THAT IS ALSO THE MECHANISM FOR THE NEGATIVE REDUCTION: renormalising to keep the book fully invested pushes weight off the 3.56 percent that is flagged and onto the 96.44 percent that is not, and the unflagged holdings carry 98.81 percent of the crashes, so the haircut moves capital toward the more crash-prone part of the book. The un-renormalised hold-cash sensitivity gives +0.595 percent, exactly the arithmetic ceiling, which is an internal consistency check rather than an independent result. A CORRECTION AGAINST MY OWN REGISTER, MADE BY MEASUREMENT BEFORE PUBLICATION: its section 6 priced the fail-open from MA28's PANEL-WIDE figures, where unflaggable rows crash at 0.8134 percent against the flaggable-and-kept 0.8928 percent, and called them marginally SAFER and the point a material mitigation. IN THE BOOK THAT IS BACKWARDS - unflaggable holdings crash at 1.055 percent against 0.643 percent, they are the MOST crash-prone bucket in the book, and they carry 31 of the 84 crashes while taking NO haircut at all. So the sizing rule is blind to the single largest crash bucket it faces and blind to a quarter of the book, which is exactly the failure the register named as the one that matters, and the register got its SIGN wrong by pricing a book-level question with panel-level data. Reported POST-HOC with NO VERDICT on MA28's own C4 precedent, and NO RATIO is quoted on the flagged bucket because one crash is not a rate. CONTROLS, all four gating and all exact: C1 reproduces the published record at max abs delta 0.000e+00; C2 PROVES my decile membership is the shipped one by reproducing quantile_backtest's own per-date series.alpha on 69 of 69 dates at max abs delta 0.000e+00, a control that exists because MB18 was burned by a re-derived construction two items ago; C3 imports build_flags from s10_accounting_veto rather than redefining it and reproduces MA28's flagged share 0.057414 and 6,542 rows exactly, with unflaggable share 0.2201; C4 the haircut is inert at 1.0x at 0.000e+00 on both exposure and return. THE POWER STATEMENT WAS WRONG BY SIX-FOLD IN THE HELPFUL DIRECTION: the register predicted resolution near 1.87pp by borrowing V2G's paired HAC SE of 0.9354pp and called the design matched to its bar with no room to spare, while the measured paired SE is 0.1106pp for an 80 percent-power MDE of 0.314pp. The reason is a perturbation-size difference I failed to account for - V2G swapped whole themes while this arm rescales 3.56 percent of holdings by half, and a paired difference between two highly correlated books is measured far more precisely. So the non-inferiority pass is GENUINELY INFORMATIVE rather than the weak undetectable the register warned of; borrowing another register's SE without checking the perturbation is comparable is B7's family again. Expectations 4 right, 2 wrong, and both misses are one miss - I put 55/45 that the top-decile flagged share of crash exposure would EXCEED the panel-wide 19.14 percent and it is sixteen times smaller, and 70/30 that the reduction would land within 3pp of the arithmetic's 6.12 percent where it is -1.44 percent; both follow from assuming the book looks like the panel.
